## Assistant

I couldn’t find `AGENTS.md` in `/home/rem/wacli`, so I’m proceeding without repo‑specific instructions.

Here’s what I found via `--help` (useful for a TUI data layer):
- `wacli chats list/show` for chat list + metadata
- `wacli messages list/show/context/search` for message pane + context jumps
- `wacli contacts search/show/alias/tags` for display names + tagging
- `wacli media download` for attachments
- `wacli sync` for live updates
- `--json` global flag for structured output

**Proposal: ranger‑style TUI layout**
- Left pane: chats list (filterable, unread counts)
- Center pane: messages for selected chat (scrollback + search)
- Right pane: chat info / participants / media stats / tags
- Bottom input: compose box + status line
- Keybindings: `j/k` move, `l` open chat, `/` search, `g/G` jump, `i` compose, `t` tag, `m` media

**Implementation approach options**
1. TUI as a separate binary that shells out to `wacli --json` (fast to build, decoupled).
2. TUI inside repo using internal packages / DB access (more control, faster, but tighter coupling).

**Open questions to lock the plan**
1. Do you want the TUI as a new subcommand (e.g., `wacli tui`) or a separate binary?
2. Should it be read‑only at first (browse/search), or include sending/editing immediately?
3. Any must‑have ranger behaviors (preview pane, bookmarks, quick filters, etc.)?

If you want, I can also inspect the Go code to see how `wacli` reads the local DB and whether there’s a clean internal API to reuse for a TUI.

To try to fetch *older* messages, use on-demand history sync requests to your **primary device** (your phone).\nREADME.md:105:- Requests are **per chat** (DM or group). `wacli` uses the *oldest locally stored message* in that chat as the anchor.\ninternal/wa/locking_regression_test.go:121:\t// We don't care whether it's c.mu or something.mu; the rule is \"don't call into whatsmeow while holding mu\".\ndocs/spec.md:3:This document defines the v1 plan for `wacli`: a WhatsApp CLI that syncs messages locally, supports fast search, sending, and contact/group management. Implementation will use `whatsmeow` under the hood.\ndocs/spec.md:12:- **Single-instance safety**: store locking to avoid multi-instance session conflicts (device/session replacement issues).\ndocs/spec.md:28:Default store: `~/.wacli` (override with `--store DIR`).\ndocs/spec.md:32:- `~/.wacli/session.db` — `whatsmeow` SQL store (device identity, keys, app-state).\ndocs/spec.md:33:- `~/.wacli/wacli.db` — our SQLite DB (messages/chats, FTS, local metadata).\ndocs/spec.md:35:- `~/.wacli/LOCK` — store lock to prevent concurrent access.\ndocs/spec.md:37:Rationale for two SQLite files: reduce coupling and keep the `whatsmeow`-owned schema separate from `wacli`’s local schema.   "stdout": "package store\n\nimport (\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"strings\"\n\t\"time\"\n)\n\nfunc (d *DB) UpsertChat(jid, kind, name string, lastTS time.Time) error {\n\tif strings.TrimSpace(kind) == \"\" {\n\t\tkind = \"unknown\"\n\t}\n\t_, err := d.sql.Exec(`\n\t\tINSERT INTO chats(jid, kind, name, last_message_ts)\n\t\tVALUES(?, ?, ?, ?)\n\t\tON CONFLICT(jid) DO UPDATE SET\n\t\t\tkind=excluded.kind,\n\t\t\tname=CASE WHEN excluded.name IS NOT NULL AND excluded.name != '' THEN excluded.name ELSE chats.name END,\n\t\t\tlast_message_ts=CASE WHEN excluded.last_message_ts > COALESCE(chats.last_message_ts, 0) THEN excluded.last_message_ts ELSE chats.last_message_ts END\n\t`, jid, kind, name, unix(lastTS))\n\treturn err\n}\n\nfunc (d *DB) ListChats(query string, limit int) ([]Chat, error) {\n\tif limit <= 0 {\n\t\tlimit = 50\n\t}\n\tq := `SELECT jid, kind, COALESCE(name,''), COALESCE(last_message_ts,0) FROM chats WHERE 1=1`\n\tvar args []interface{}\n\tif strings.TrimSpace(query) != \"\" {\n\t\tq += ` AND (LOWER(name) LIKE LOWER(?) OR LOWER(jid) LIKE LOWER(?))`\n\t\tneedle := \"%\" + query + \"%\"\n\t\targs = append(args, needle, needle)\n\t}\n\tq += ` ORDER BY last_message_ts DESC LIMIT ?`\n\targs = append(args, limit)\n\n\trows, err := d.sql.Query(q, args...)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tdefer rows.Close()\n\n\tvar out []Chat\n\tfor rows.Next() {\n\t\tvar c Chat\n\t\tvar ts int64\n\t\tif err := rows.Scan(&c.JID, &c.Kind, &c.Name, &ts); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\tc.LastMessageTS = fromUnix(ts)\n\t\tout = append(out, c)\n\t}\n\treturn out, rows.Err()\n}\n\nfunc (d *DB) GetChat(jid string) (Chat, error) {\n\trow := d.sql.QueryRow(`SELECT jid, kind, COALESCE(name,''), COALESCE(last_message_ts,0) FROM chats WHERE jid = ?`, jid)\n\tvar c Chat\n\tvar ts int64\n\tif err := row.Scan(&c.JID, &c.Kind, &c.Name, &ts); err != nil {\n\t\treturn Chat{}, err\n\t}\n\tc.LastMessageTS = fromUnix(ts)\n\treturn c, nil\n}\n\nfunc (d *DB) SearchContacts(query string, limit int) ([]Contact, error) {\n\tif strings.TrimSpace(query) == \"\" {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"query is required\")\n\t}\n\tif limit <= 0 {\n\t\tlimit = 50\n\t}\n\tq := `\n\t\tSELECT c.jid,\n\t\t       COALESCE(c.phone,''),\n\t\t       COALESCE(NULLIF(a.alias,''), ''),\n\t\t       COALESCE(NULLIF(c.full_name,''), NULLIF(c.push_name,''), NULLIF(c.business_name,''), NULLIF(c.first_name,''), ''),\n\t\t       c.updated_at\n\t\tFROM contacts c\n\t\tLEFT JOIN contact_aliases a ON a.jid = c.jid\n\t\tWHERE LOWER(COALESCE(a.alias,'')) LIKE LOWER(?) OR LOWER(COALESCE(c.full_name,'')) LIKE LOWER(?) OR LOWER(COALESCE(c.push_name,'')) LIKE LOWER(?) OR LOWER(COALESCE(c.phone,'')) LIKE LOWER(?) OR LOWER(c.jid) LIKE LOWER(?)\n\t\tORDER BY COALESCE(NULLIF(a.alias,''), NULLIF(c.full_name,''), NULLIF(c.push_name,''), c.jid)\n\t\tLIMIT ?`\n\tneedle := \"%\" + query + \"%\"\n\trows, err := d.sql.Query(q, needle, needle, needle, needle, needle, limit)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tdefer rows.Close()\n\n\tvar out []Contact\n\tfor rows.Next() {\n\t\tvar c Contact\n\t\tvar updated int64\n\t\tif err := rows.Scan(&c.JID, &c.Phone, &c.Alias, &c.Name, &updated); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\tc.UpdatedAt = fromUnix(updated)\n\t\tout = append(out, c)\n\t}\n\treturn out, rows.Err()\n}\n\nfunc (d *DB) GetContact(jid string) (Contact, error) {\n\trow := d.sql.QueryRow(`\n\t\tSELECT c.jid,\n\t\t       COALESCE(c.phone,''),\n\t\t       COALESCE(NULLIF(a.alias,''), ''),\n\t\t       COALESCE(NULLIF(c.full_name,''), NULLIF(c.push_name,''), NULLIF(c.business_name,''), NULLIF(c.first_name,''), ''),\n\t\t       c.updated_at\n\t\tFROM contacts c\n\t\tLEFT JOIN contact_aliases a ON a.jid = c.jid\n\t\tWHERE c.jid = ?\n\t`, jid)\n\tvar c Contact\n\tvar updated int64\n\tif err := row.Scan(&c.JID, &c.Phone, &c.Alias, &c.Name, &updated); err != nil {\n\t\treturn Contact{}, err\n\t}\n\tc.UpdatedAt = fromUnix(updated)\n\ttags, _ := d.ListTags(jid)\n\tc.Tags = tags\n\treturn c, nil\n}\n\nfunc (d *DB) ListTags(jid string) ([]string, error) {\n\trows, err := d.sql.Query(`SELECT tag FROM contact_tags WHERE jid = ? ORDER BY tag`, jid)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tdefer rows.Close()\n\n\tvar tags []string\n\tfor rows.Next() {\n\t\tvar tag string\n\t\tif err := rows.Scan(&tag); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\ttags = append(tags, tag)\n\t}\n\treturn tags, rows.Err()\n}\n\nfunc (d *DB) UpsertContact(jid, phone, pushName, fullName, firstName, businessName string) error {\n\tnow := time.Now().UTC().Unix()\n\t_, err := d.sql.Exec(`\n\t\tINSERT INTO contacts(jid, phone, push_name, full_name, first_name, business_name, updated_at)\n\t\tVALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)\n\t\tON CONFLICT(jid) DO UPDATE SET\n\t\t\tphone=COALESCE(NULLIF(excluded.phone,''), contacts.phone),\n\t\t\tpush_name=COALESCE(NULLIF(excluded.push_name,''), contacts.push_name),\n\t\t\tfull_name=COALESCE(NULLIF(excluded.full_name,''), contacts.full_name),\n\t\t\tfirst_name=COALESCE(NULLIF(excluded.first_name,''), contacts.first_name),\n\t\t\tbusiness_name=COALESCE(NULLIF(excluded.business_name,''), contacts.business_name),\n\t\t\tupdated_at=excluded.updated_at\n\t`, jid, phone, pushName, fullName, firstName, businessName, now)\n\treturn err\n}\n\nfunc (d *DB) UpsertGroup(jid, name, ownerJID string, created time.Time) error {\n\tnow := time.Now().UTC().Unix()\n\t_, err := d.sql.Exec(`\n\t\tINSERT INTO groups(jid, name, owner_jid, created_ts, updated_at)\n\t\tVALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?)\n\t\tON CONFLICT(jid) DO UPDATE SET\n\t\t\tname=COALESCE(NULLIF(excluded.name,''), groups.name),\n\t\t\towner_jid=COALESCE(NULLIF(excluded.owner_jid,''), groups.owner_jid),\n\t\t\tcreated_ts=COALESCE(NULLIF(excluded.created_ts,0), groups.created_ts),\n\t\t\tupdated_at=excluded.updated_at\n\t`, jid, name, ownerJID, unix(created), now)\n\treturn err\n}\n\nfunc (d *DB) ReplaceGroupParticipants(groupJID string, participants []GroupParticipant) (err error) {\n\ttx, err := d.sql.Begin()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\tdefer func() {\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t_ = tx.Rollback()\n\t\t}\n\t}()\n\n\tif _, err = tx.Exec(`DELETE FROM group_participants WHERE group_jid = ?`, groupJID); err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\tstmt, err := tx.Prepare(`INSERT INTO group_participants(group_jid, user_jid, role, updated_at) VALUES(?, ?, ?, ?)`)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\tdefer stmt.Close()\n\n\tnow := time.Now().UTC()\n\tfor _, participant := range participants {\n\t\trole := strings.TrimSpace(participant.Role)\n\t\tif role == \"\" {\n\t\t\trole = \"member\"\n\t\t}\n\t\tif _, err = stmt.Exec(groupJID, participant.UserJID, role, unix(now)); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn tx.Commit()\n}\n\nfunc (d *DB) ListGroups(query string, limit int) ([]Group, error) {\n\tif limit <= 0 {\n\t\tlimit = 50\n\t}\n\tq := `SELECT jid, COALESCE(name,''), COALESCE(owner_jid,''), COALESCE(created_ts,0), updated_at FROM groups WHERE 1=1`\n\tvar args []interface{}\n\tif strings.TrimSpace(query) != \"\" {\n\t\tneedle := \"%\" + query + \"%\"\n\t\tq += ` AND (LOWER(name) LIKE LOWER(?) OR LOWER(jid) LIKE LOWER(?))`\n\t\targs = append(args, needle, needle)\n\t}\n\tq += ` ORDER BY COALESCE(created_ts,0) DESC LIMIT ?`\n\targs = append(args, limit)\n\n\trows, err := d.sql.Query(q, args...)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tdefer rows.Close()\n\n\tvar out []Group\n\tfor rows.Next() {\n\t\tvar g Group\n\t\tvar created, updated int64\n\t\tif err := rows.Scan(&g.JID, &g.Name, &g.OwnerJID, &created, &updated); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\tg.CreatedAt = fromUnix(created)\n\t\tg.UpdatedAt = fromUnix(updated)\n\t\tout = append(out, g)\n\t}\n\treturn out, rows.Err()\n}\n\nfunc (d *DB) SetAlias(jid, alias string) error {\n\talias = strings.TrimSpace(alias)\n\tif alias == \"\" {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"alias is required\")\n\t}\n\tnow := time.Now().UTC().Unix()\n\t_, err := d.sql.Exec(`\n\t\tINSERT INTO contact_aliases(jid, alias, notes, updated_at)\n\t\tVALUES (?, ?, NULL, ?)\n\t\tON CONFLICT(jid) DO UPDATE SET alias=excluded.alias, updated_at=excluded.updated_at\n\t`, jid, alias, now)\n\treturn err\n}\n\nfunc (d *DB) RemoveAlias(jid string) error {\n\t_, err := d.sql.Exec(`DELETE FROM contact_aliases WHERE jid = ?`, jid)\n\treturn err\n}\n",

## Assistant

Here’s what I found in the Go code about local DB access and whether there’s a clean API you can reuse for a TUI.

**How `wacli` reads the local DB**
- Local DB access is centralized in `internal/store` which wraps SQLite with a small typed API.
  - `Open(path)` opens the sqlite DB, runs pragmas, and applies migrations. `internal/store/db.go` handles this. `internal/store/db.go:1`
  - Migrations define the full schema for `wacli.db` (messages/chats/contacts/groups/fts). `internal/store/migrations.go:1`
  - Data access methods are in:
    - `internal/store/messages.go` (list, get, search, context)
    - `internal/store/chats_contacts_groups.go` (chats, contacts, groups, tags, aliases)
    - `internal/store/search.go` (FTS/LIKE message search)
- The CLI commands instantiate an `app.App`, then call `a.DB().ListMessages/SearchMessages/...`. `cmd/wacli/messages.go` uses the store API directly. `cmd/wacli/messages.go:1`
- `app.New` opens `wacli.db` located at `<storeDir>/wacli.db`. `internal/app/app.go:1`
- Locking:
  - Only commands that need WhatsApp session or network use `lock.Acquire`. DB-only commands (like `messages list/search/show`) do not acquire the lock. `cmd/wacli/root.go:1`

**Is there a clean internal API you can reuse for a TUI?**
Yes, but with a key limitation:
- The clean API is `internal/store.DB` and `internal/app.App`. It’s stable enough for a TUI inside the same module.
- Because it is under `internal/`, you cannot import it from a separate external module. This is a Go visibility rule.
  - So: if you build the TUI **inside this repo** (e.g., `cmd/wacli-tui` or `cmd/wacli` subcommand), you can reuse `internal/store` and `internal/app` directly.
  - If you want a **separate repo/binary**, you should either:
    1. Shell out to `wacli --json`, or
    2. Open SQLite directly (read-only) using the schema from `internal/store/migrations.go`, or
    3. Refactor/move the store package out of `internal/` to a public package (bigger change).

**Relevant entry points**
- DB open / migrations: `internal/store/db.go:1`, `internal/store/migrations.go:1`
- Queries for TUI panes:
  - Chats list: `internal/store/chats_contacts_groups.go:1`
  - Messages list/search/context: `internal/store/messages.go:1`, `internal/store/search.go:1`
- App wiring for store path + WA session: `internal/app/app.go:1`

If you want, I can trace the data flow for sync (how messages get stored) or map out which TUI screen can be built from which store methods.

## User

first i want you to create a AGENTS.md then i want you to go on exploring trace the data flow for sync (how messages get stored) or map out which TUI screen can be built from which store methods.

**Tool Call: Run cat > AGENTS.md <<'EOF'
# AGENTS.md

This repository is the `wacli` WhatsApp CLI (Go). Use these local instructions when working here.

## Basics
- Default store: `~/.wacli` (override with `--store`).
- There are two SQLite DBs in the store dir:
  - `session.db` (whatsmeow session/auth state)
  - `wacli.db` (local app DB for chats/messages/contacts/groups)
- Many commands use `--json` to emit machine-readable output.

## Code Map
- CLI commands: `cmd/wacli`
- App wiring: `internal/app`
- WhatsApp client wrapper: `internal/wa`
- Local DB access + schema: `internal/store`
- Store locking: `internal/lock`
- Output formatting: `internal/out`

## Dev Notes
- Use `rg` to search.
- Avoid destructive git commands.
- Prefer small, scoped changes.

## Testing
- Go tests live in `internal/...` (use `go test ./...` when needed).
EOF**

s != \"\" {\n\t\tmsg.SenderJID = s\n\t}\n\n\textractWAProto(evt.Message, &msg)\n\treturn msg\n}\n\nfunc ParseHistoryMessage(chatJID string, hist *waProto.WebMessageInfo) ParsedMessage {\n\tvar chat types.JID\n\tif parsed, err := types.ParseJID(chatJID); err == nil {\n\t\tchat = parsed\n\t}\n\n\tpm := ParsedMessage{\n\t\tChat:      chat,\n\t\tID:        hist.GetKey().GetID(),\n\t\tTimestamp: time.Unix(int64(hist.GetMessageTimestamp()), 0).UTC(),\n\t\tFromMe:    hist.GetKey().GetFromMe(),\n\t}\n\n\tsender := strings.TrimSpace(hist.GetKey().GetParticipant())\n\tif sender == \"\" {\n\t\tsender = strings.TrimSpace(hist.GetKey().GetRemoteJID())\n\t}\n\tpm.SenderJID = sender\n\n\tif hist.GetMessage() != nil {\n\t\textractWAProto(hist.GetMessage(), &pm)\n\t}\n\treturn pm\n}\n\nfunc extractWAProto(m *waProto.Message, pm *ParsedMessage) {\n\tif m == nil || pm == nil {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tif reaction := m.GetReactionMessage(); encReaction != nil {\n\t\tif key := encReaction.GetTargetMessageKey(); key != nil {\n\t\t\tpm.ReactionToID = key.GetID()\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tswitch {\n\tcase m.GetConversation() != \"\":\n\t\tpm.Text = m.GetConversation()\n\tcase m.GetExtendedTextMessage() != nil:\n\t\tpm.Text = m.GetExtendedTextMessage().GetText()\n\t}\n\n\tif img := m.GetImageMessage(); img != nil {\n\t\tif pm.Text == \"\" {\n\t\t\tpm.Text = img.GetCaption()\n\t\t}\n\t\tpm.Media = &Media{\n\t\t\tType:          \"image\",\n\t\t\tCaption:       img.GetCaption(),\n\t\t\tMimeType:      img.GetMimetype(),\n\t\t\tDirectPath:    img.GetDirectPath(),\n\t\t\tMediaKey:      clone(img.GetMediaKey()),\n\t\t\tFileSHA256:    clone(img.GetFileSHA256()),\n\t\t\tFileEncSHA256: clone(img.GetFileEncSHA256()),\n\t\t\tFileLength:    img.GetFileLength(),\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tif vid := m.GetVideoMessage(); vid != nil {\n\t\tif pm.Text == \"\" {\n\t\t\tpm.Text = vid.GetCaption()\n\t\t}\n\t\tmediaType := \"video\"\n\t\tif vid.GetGifPlayback() {\n\t\t\tmediaType = \"gif\"\n\t\t}\n\t\tpm.Media = &Media{\n\t\t\tType:          mediaType,\n\t\t\tCaption:       vid.GetCaption(),\n\t\t\tMimeType:      vid.GetMimetype(),\n\t\t\tDirectPath:    vid.GetDirectPath(),\n\t\t\tMediaKey:      clone(vid.GetMediaKey()),\n\t\t\tFileSHA256:    clone(vid.GetFileSHA256()),\n\t\t\tFileEncSHA256: clone(vid.GetFileEncSHA256()),\n\t\t\tFileLength:    vid.GetFileLength(),\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tif aud := m.GetAudioMessage(); aud != nil {\n\t\tif pm.Text == \"\" {\n\t\t\tpm.Text = \"[Audio]\"\n\t\t}\n\t\tpm.Media = &Media{\n\t\t\tType:          \"audio\",\n\t\t\tCaption:       pm.Text,\n\t\t\tMimeType:      aud.GetMimetype(),\n\t\t\tDirectPath:    aud.GetDirectPath(),\n\t\t\tMediaKey:      clone(aud.GetMediaKey()),\n\t\t\tFileSHA256:    clone(aud.GetFileSHA256()),\n\t\t\tFileEncSHA256: clone(aud.GetFileEncSHA256()),\n\t\t\tFileLength:    aud.GetFileLength(),\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tif doc := m.GetDocumentMessage(); ## Assistant

AGENTS.md created at `/home/rem/wacli/AGENTS.md`.

**Sync data flow (how messages get stored)**
- `wacli sync` opens the app with store lock, ensures auth, then calls `App.Sync`. `cmd/wacli/sync.go:1`
- `App.Sync` registers a WhatsApp event handler; it processes live `events.Message` and `events.HistorySync` events. `internal/app/sync.go:41`
- Each event is parsed into a `wa.ParsedMessage` via `wa.ParseLiveMessage` or `wa.ParseHistoryMessage`, which extracts text/media/reply/reaction fields from the proto. `internal/wa/messages.go:39`
- Parsed messages are persisted via `storeParsedMessage`, which:
  - Upserts chat metadata.
  - Upserts contacts and group metadata (best‑effort).
  - Builds `display_text` (reply/reaction text).
  - Upserts the message row (including media fields). `internal/app/sync.go:226`
- The actual DB writes are in `internal/store` (`UpsertChat`, `UpsertContact`, `UpsertGroup`, `ReplaceGroupParticipants`, `UpsertMessage`). `internal/store/chats_contacts_groups.go:9`, `internal/store/messages.go:30`

**TUI screen mapping to store methods**
- Chat list pane: `ListChats` (query + limit) and `GetChat` for details. `internal/store/chats_contacts_groups.go:24`
- Messages pane: `ListMessages` for scrollback, `MessageContext` for thread jumps, `GetMessage` for focused view. `internal/store/messages.go:68`
- Search pane: `SearchMessages` (FTS if enabled, otherwise LIKE). `internal/store/search.go:19`
- Contacts pane / name resolution: `SearchContacts`, `GetContact`, `ListTags`. `internal/store/chats_contacts_groups.go:68`
- Groups pane: `ListGroups` and participants from `ReplaceGroupParticipants` data (TUI would need a read method for participants; currently only write path exists). `internal/store/chats_contacts_groups.go:176`
- Media pane: `GetMediaDownloadInfo`/`MarkMediaDownloaded` live in `internal/store/media.go` (I can map these next if you want to surface media previews).

If you want, I can also trace the WhatsApp client wrapper (`internal/wa/client.go`) to show how events and session store are wired, or sketch a minimal read‑only TUI flow that uses only `internal/store` and avoids WhatsApp connections.

## User

tell me what would be better, 
1. a seperate tui binary which uses --json flag on wacli
2. or wacli tui as the subcommand

## Assistant

Short answer: `wacli tui` as a subcommand is better for performance and UX, unless you explicitly want a separate repo/binary.

Why `wacli tui` (subcommand) is better:
- Direct access to `internal/store` and `internal/app` without JSON parsing or shelling out.
- Easier to share config, store dir handling, and locking semantics.
- Single install/update for users.
- Fewer moving parts, less latency on list/search.

When a separate binary makes sense:
- You want a different release cadence or independent distribution.
- You want to keep the TUI language/toolkit separate from Go.
- You don’t want to depend on `internal/` packages (or prefer strict CLI boundary).

Given your ranger‑style TUI goal and that we’re already in this repo, I’d recommend `wacli tui` as the subcommand. If you want a clean separation later, we can refactor store access into a public package and still keep it as a subcommand.
